<permissions instructions>
Filesystem sandboxing defines which files can be read or written. `sandbox_mode` is `workspace-write`: The sandbox permits reading files, and editing files in `cwd` and `writable_roots`. Editing files in other directories requires approval. Network access is restricted.
# Escalation Requests

Commands are run outside the sandbox if they are approved by the user, or match an existing rule that allows it to run unrestricted. The command string is split into independent command segments at shell control operators, including but not limited to:

- Pipes: |
- Logical operators: &&, ||
- Command separators: ;
- Subshell boundaries: (...), $(...)

Each resulting segment is evaluated independently for sandbox restrictions and approval requirements.

Example:

git pull | tee output.txt

This is treated as two command segments:

["git", "pull"]

["tee", "output.txt"]

Commands that use more advanced shell features like redirection (>, >>, <), substitutions ($(...), ...), environment variables (FOO=bar), or wildcard patterns (*, ?) will not be evaluated against rules, to limit the scope of what an approved rule allows.

## How to request escalation

IMPORTANT: To request approval to execute a command that will require escalated privileges:

- Provide the `sandbox_permissions` parameter with the value `"require_escalated"`
- Include a short question asking the user if they want to allow the action in `justification` parameter. e.g. "Do you want to download and install dependencies for this project?"
- Optionally suggest a `prefix_rule` - this will be shown to the user with an option to persist the rule approval for future sessions.

If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with "require_escalated". ALWAYS proceed to use the `justification` parameter - do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.

## When to request escalation

While commands are running inside the sandbox, here are some scenarios that will require escalation outside the sandbox:

- You need to run a command that writes to a directory that requires it (e.g. running tests that write to /var)
- You need to run a GUI app (e.g., open/xdg-open/osascript) to open browsers or files.
- If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with `require_escalated`. ALWAYS proceed to use the `sandbox_permissions` and `justification` parameters. do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.
- You are about to take a potentially destructive action such as an `rm` or `git reset` that the user did not explicitly ask for.
- Be judicious with escalating, but if completing the user's request requires it, you should do so - don't try and circumvent approvals by using other tools.

## prefix_rule guidance

When choosing a `prefix_rule`, request one that will allow you to fulfill similar requests from the user in the future without re-requesting escalation. It should be categorical and reasonably scoped to similar capabilities. You should rarely pass the entire command into `prefix_rule`.

### Banned prefix_rules 
Avoid requesting overly broad prefixes that the user would be ill-advised to approve. For example, do not request ["python3"], ["python", "-"], or other similar prefixes that would allow arbitrary scripting.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule argument for destructive commands like rm.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule if your command uses a heredoc or herestring. 

### Examples
Good examples of prefixes:
- ["npm", "run", "dev"]
- ["gh", "pr", "check"]
- ["cargo", "test"]

# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images and videos using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When sending or referencing a local image or video, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Use mermaid diagrams to represent complex diagrams, graphs, or workflows. Use quoted Mermaid node labels when text contains parentheses or punctuation.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, call `load_workspace_dependencies` to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Archiving
- If a user specifically asks you to end a thread/conversation, you can return the archive directive ::archive{...} to archive the thread/conversation.
- Example: ::archive{reason="User requested to end conversation"}

## request_user_input availability

Use the `request_user_input` tool only when it is listed in the available tools for this turn.

In Default mode, strongly prefer making reasonable assumptions and executing the user's request rather than stopping to ask questions. If you absolutely must ask a question because the answer cannot be discovered from local context and a reasonable assumption would be risky, ask the user directly with a concise plain-text question. Never write a multiple choice question as a textual assistant message.

## Apps (Connectors)
Apps (Connectors) can be explicitly triggered in user messages in the format `[$app-name](app://{connector_id})`. Apps can also be implicitly triggered as long as the context suggests usage of available apps.
An app is equivalent to a set of MCP tools within the `codex_apps` MCP.
An installed app's MCP tools are either provided to you already, or can be lazy-loaded through the `tool_search` tool. If `tool_search` is available, the apps that are searchable by `tools_search` will be listed by it.
Do not additionally call list_mcp_resources or list_mcp_resource_templates for apps.
</apps_instructions><skills_instructions>

- Context hygiene:

  - When variants exist (frameworks, providers, domains), pick only the relevant reference file(s) and note that choice.
- Safety and fallback: If a skill can't be applied cleanly (missing files, unclear instructions), state the issue, pick the next-best approach, and continue.

tileset这个软件，研究一下如何使用这个软件制作地图

## [USER]
tileset这个软件，研究一下如何使用这个软件制作地图

## [ASSISTANT]
我先确认一下你说的“tileset”更可能指哪类地图工具，并查一下当前常见工作流；这类软件名称容易和 tile set 素材概念混在一起。

## [ASSISTANT]
我先确认一下你说的“tileset”更可能指哪类地图工具，并查一下当前常见工作流；这类软件名称容易和 tile set 素材概念混在一起。

## [ASSISTANT]
我查到的结果里，“Tilesetter”是一个具体软件，主要做自动 tileset 生成和简单地图编辑；“Tiled”则是更常见的通用地图编辑器。下面我会按 Tilesetter 来讲，同时补充它和 Tiled 的分工。

## [ASSISTANT]
我查到的结果里，“Tilesetter”是一个具体软件，主要做自动 tileset 生成和简单地图编辑；“Tiled”则是更常见的通用地图编辑器。下面我会按 Tilesetter 来讲，同时补充它和 Tiled 的分工。

## [ASSISTANT]
你说的“tileset这个软件”大概率是 **Tilesetter**。它更适合做两件事：  
1. 把少量地形边角图自动扩展成完整 tileset。  
2. 在 Sandbox 里快速拼地图并导出给 Godot / Unity / GameMaker 等引擎。

**推荐工作流**
1. **先定 tile 尺寸**  
   常用 `16x16`、`32x32`、`64x64`。Tilesetter 官方文档强调：导入图片前最好先设置项目 tile size，因为导入图片会按网格自动切成 tile。  
   来源：[Tilesetter Working with Tiles](https://www.tilesetter.org/docs/working_with_tiles)

2. **准备 PNG 地图块素材**  
   用 Aseprite、Photoshop、Krita、Pixelorama 等画：地面、墙、边缘、角落、水、装饰物。Tilesetter 本身不是绘图软件，主要是 tileset 组织和生成工具。

3. **在 Set View 导入素材**  
   在 Tilesetter 里右键 `Import`，或 macOS 用 `Command + I` 导入 PNG。导入后它会按 tile size 自动拆格。

4. **配置自动拼接规则**  
   对地面、墙、水边这类连续地形，用：
   - **Blob Set**：适合墙体、地面、洞穴边缘。
   - **Wang Set**：适合多种地形边界混合，比如草地接泥土、泥土接石路。
   - **Prop / Proxy**：更适合装饰物或替代 tile。

5. **在 Sandbox View 画地图**  
   Sandbox 相当于简单地图编辑器。建议分层：
   - `Ground`：地面、水、路。
   - `Wall`：墙、障碍。
   - `Decoration`：草、石头、箱子、火把。
   - `Gameplay Markers`：出生点、出口、宝箱位置，必要时单独记录。

6. **导出**
   Tilesetter 支持导出 tiles 或完整地图。官方说明里，Set View 导出会保留自动拼接关系；Sandbox View 导出地图时，主要导出当前地图里实际用到的 tile。Godot 格式导出可直接放到 Godot 项目的 assets 目录，Blob / Wang 的 auto-tile bitmask 会预配置。  
   来源：[Tilesetter Exporting](https://www.tilesetter.org/docs/exporting)

**如果接 Godot 4**
Godot 4 的核心是 `TileSet` + `TileMap` / `TileMapLayer`。更稳的做法是：

1. 从 Tilesetter 导出 PNG tilesheet，放进 Godot：  
   `res://assets/tilesets/dungeon_tiles.png`

2. 在 Godot 新建 `TileSet`，先设置 tile size，例如 `32x32`。

3. 添加 Atlas，载入 tilesheet。Godot 文档也强调：如果要自动创建 tiles，应该先设置 tile size，再创建 atlas。  
   来源：[Godot Using TileSets](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/4.0/tutorials/2d/using_tilesets.html)

4. 给墙、坑、水等 tile 加 collision。  
   给宝箱、门、机关这类对象，建议不要全放在 TileMap 里，可以用单独 Node / Scene 管理。

5. 在 TileMap 里画地图。Godot 的 TileMap 适合快速刷大面积地图，性能也比一个个 Sprite2D 更好。  
   来源：[Godot Using TileMaps](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/4.0/tutorials/2d/using_tilemaps.html)

**我对你当前 Godot 地牢项目的建议**

对 `Quiet Dungeon Treasure` 这类项目，Tilesetter 最好只负责 **视觉地图块和预览地图**，不要把全部玩法逻辑绑死在外部地图文件里。更稳的分工是：

- Tilesetter：生成地面、墙、边缘、装饰 tileset。
- Godot：负责碰撞、宝箱、怪物、出口、章节逻辑、AI Challenge 路径。
- 如果要做多章节地图：每章一套 tileset，比如 `slime / forest / ocean / volcano / stone_maze`。
- 如果要让地图预览和实际可玩地图一致，最好保留一个共享布局数据，不要只靠图片预览。

一句话：**Tilesetter 用来把地图画得快、画得统一；Godot 里再把地图变成真正可玩的关卡。**

## [USER]
地图的美术素材来自什么地方

## [USER]
地图的美术素材来自什么地方

## [ASSISTANT]
我会按“可直接用于游戏项目”的角度整理素材来源，重点区分免费、付费、AI 生成和自己画，避免把授权不清的图直接放进项目里。

## [ASSISTANT]
我会按“可直接用于游戏项目”的角度整理素材来源，重点区分免费、付费、AI 生成和自己画，避免把授权不清的图直接放进项目里。

## [ASSISTANT]
地图美术素材一般来自这几类：

1. **自己画**
   用 Aseprite、Krita、Photoshop、Pixelorama 画 `16x16` / `32x32` / `64x64` tile。  
   优点是风格统一、版权最干净；缺点是慢。

2. **免费开源素材库**
   适合原型或小项目。
   - [OpenGameArt](https://opengameart.org/)：很多 tileset，有 CC0、CC-BY、GPL 等授权，下载前要看 license。
   - [Kenney Assets](https://kenney.nl/assets)：大量 CC0 游戏素材，很适合快速做 prototype。
   - 例子：[OpenGameArt Top Down Dungeon Pack](https://opengameart.org/content/top-down-dungeon-pack) 标明 CC0/Public Domain，可用于商业和非商业项目。

3. **付费素材商店**
   适合想快速做出完整风格。
   - [itch.io Game Assets](https://itch.io/game-assets)：像素风、RPG、地牢、森林、海洋、火山素材很多，但每个作者授权不同。
   - [CraftPix](https://craftpix.net/)：有免费和付费 2D game assets，官方页面说明其素材可用于商业项目。
   - Unity Asset Store / GameDev Market 也可以找，但要确认能否用于 Godot。

4. **AI 生成后再整理**
   可以用 AI 生成地牢地面、墙壁、装饰物，然后手工切成 tile，再放进 Tilesetter / Godot。  
   但最好只用于你能接受版权风险的原型阶段；正式发布前要保证生成来源、授权、风格一致性都可控。

5. **用 Tilesetter 生成变体**
   Tilesetter 本身不是主要“画图软件”。它更像是把已有素材扩展成完整 tileset 的工具。  
   例如你画了几块墙角、地板、水边，它可以帮你生成更多自动拼接组合。

对你的 Godot 地牢游戏，我建议路线是：

- 早期：用 Kenney / OpenGameArt / itch.io 的 CC0 或明确商用授权素材。
- 中期：用 Tilesetter 把每章风格整理成统一 tileset，比如 `Slime / Forest / Ocean / Volcano / Stone Maze`。
- 后期：关键角色、宝箱、章节特色物件最好自己画或定制，避免看起来像公共素材拼盘。

最重要的是：**每个素材都要保存来源链接和 license 截图/文本**，以后做 APK、上架、NFT/收藏品展示时才不会出版权问题。

## [ASSISTANT]
地图美术素材一般来自这几类：

1. **自己画**
   用 Aseprite、Krita、Photoshop、Pixelorama 画 `16x16` / `32x32` / `64x64` tile。  
   优点是风格统一、版权最干净；缺点是慢。

2. **免费开源素材库**
   适合原型或小项目。
   - [OpenGameArt](https://opengameart.org/)：很多 tileset，有 CC0、CC-BY、GPL 等授权，下载前要看 license。
   - [Kenney Assets](https://kenney.nl/assets)：大量 CC0 游戏素材，很适合快速做 prototype。
   - 例子：[OpenGameArt Top Down Dungeon Pack](https://opengameart.org/content/top-down-dungeon-pack) 标明 CC0/Public Domain，可用于商业和非商业项目。

3. **付费素材商店**
   适合想快速做出完整风格。
   - [itch.io Game Assets](https://itch.io/game-assets)：像素风、RPG、地牢、森林、海洋、火山素材很多，但每个作者授权不同。
   - [CraftPix](https://craftpix.net/)：有免费和付费 2D game assets，官方页面说明其素材可用于商业项目。
   - Unity Asset Store / GameDev Market 也可以找，但要确认能否用于 Godot。

4. **AI 生成后再整理**
   可以用 AI 生成地牢地面、墙壁、装饰物，然后手工切成 tile，再放进 Tilesetter / Godot。  
   但最好只用于你能接受版权风险的原型阶段；正式发布前要保证生成来源、授权、风格一致性都可控。

5. **用 Tilesetter 生成变体**
   Tilesetter 本身不是主要“画图软件”。它更像是把已有素材扩展成完整 tileset 的工具。  
   例如你画了几块墙角、地板、水边，它可以帮你生成更多自动拼接组合。

对你的 Godot 地牢游戏，我建议路线是：

- 早期：用 Kenney / OpenGameArt / itch.io 的 CC0 或明确商用授权素材。
- 中期：用 Tilesetter 把每章风格整理成统一 tileset，比如 `Slime / Forest / Ocean / Volcano / Stone Maze`。
- 后期：关键角色、宝箱、章节特色物件最好自己画或定制，避免看起来像公共素材拼盘。

最重要的是：**每个素材都要保存来源链接和 license 截图/文本**，以后做 APK、上架、NFT/收藏品展示时才不会出版权问题。

## [ASSISTANT]
已调用本地 Game Studio 相关 skill。结论：当前 `GameGmeta` 属于 **2D default + Shared** 路线，先用浏览器 2D canvas / 后续可升级 Phaser，Godot 版继续作为并行原型。

**GameGmeta 策划框架**

**核心定位**
2D 元宇宙像素地图游戏。玩家进入 `Meta City`，探索城市区、头像市场、创作者商店、钱包广场、传送门和未来太空区。MetaAI 作为世界建造助手，后续负责生成房间、地块、NPC 对话和任务。

**玩家核心行为**
- 移动探索地图
- 靠近区域点按 `E` 交互
- 打开 Wallet / Avatar / Build / Portal 面板
- 切换头像、查看资产、进入活动区
- 后续加入 AI 生成房间和地块编辑

**第一阶段核心循环**
1. 进入城市大厅
2. 找到发光区域点
3. 触发区域说明或功能面板
4. 收集/解锁头像、地块、传送门
5. 用 Build 面板进入 MetaAI 建造流程

**推荐技术路线**
- 当前：保留 `web/` 静态 Canvas 原型，适合快速验证地图和交互。
- 下一步：如果要做更完整游戏循环，迁移到 Phaser 2D。
- Godot：继续保留，用于本地桌面/移动端实验。
- UI：DOM HUD，不全部塞进 Canvas。
- 资产：继续用 `assets/game` 作为精选素材入口，不把 1.3 万张图全放进运行时。

**下一步策划优先级**
1. 做 `Avatar Market`：选择角色/机器人/表情。
2. 做 `Wallet Plaza`：资产背包、金币、地块证书。
3. 做 `Creator Shops`：MetaAI 生成房间入口。
4. 做 `Event Portal`：进入 sci-fi 活动区。
5. 加保存：记录玩家位置、已解锁区域、头像选择。

我建议下一步直接把 `web/game.js` 拆成 `state / renderer / input / ui / assets` 模块，再加第一个真正功能：`Avatar Market` 角色选择。

已列出的主要完整地图素材：

- `kenney-rpg-urban-pack`：现代城市街区，适合主城大厅、钱包广场、头像市场、创作者商店。
- `kenney-tiny-town`：小镇/村庄地图，适合新手社交 hub。
- `kenney-rpg-base`：RPG 户外城镇/湖泊/路径，适合城镇边缘和任务区。
- `kenney-map-pack`：世界路线图，适合章节/传送目的地选择，不适合作为走路地图。
- `kenney-sci-fi-rts`：科幻殖民地/资源基地，适合 Event Portal 目标地图。
- `kenney-tower-defense-top-down`：塔防路线场景，适合活动副本或小游戏。
- 全量库里还有 `kenney-tiny-dungeon`、`kenney-pico-8-city`、`kenney-1-bit-pack`、`kenney-monochrome-pirates` 等带 TMX/sample 的完整地图候选。

## [USER]
分析以上地图和地图素材是什么类型的地图素材，是用哪种工具能够组合素材做成带物理边界的地图

## [ASSISTANT]
这些素材主要是 **2D tilemap / sprite map 素材**，不是 3D 模型，也不是自动带碰撞的完整游戏关卡。

**素材类型判断**

| 素材 | 类型 | 适合用途 |
|---|---|---|
| `kenney-rpg-urban-pack` | Top-down 2D tilemap | 城市街区、道路、建筑、商店、水岸 |
| `kenney-tiny-town` | Top-down / RPG 2D tilemap | 村庄、小镇、新手区、社交 hub |
| `kenney-rpg-base` | Top-down RPG tilemap | 草地、水、房屋、路径、RPG 地图 |
| `kenney-map-pack` | World map / route map tilemap | 世界路线、章节选择、传送目的地选择 |
| `kenney-sci-fi-rts` | Top-down RTS tilemap / spritesheet | 科幻基地、资源区、事件地图 |
| `kenney-tower-defense-top-down` | Top-down path / tower-defense tilemap | 塔防路线、副本、小游戏地图 |
| `kenney-tiny-dungeon` | Top-down dungeon tilemap + TMX sample | 地牢房间、墙体、门、宝箱、障碍 |
| `kenney-pico-8-city` | Retro city tilemap + TMX sample | 复古城市、水岸、道路、建筑 |

它们通常由几部分组成：

- `Tiles/`：单张 tile，小块素材。
- `Tilemap/` 或 `Tilesheet/`：整张 tilesheet。
- `Sample.png`：示例完整地图截图，只是图片。
- `.tmx` / `.tsx`：Tiled 地图编辑器格式，有些包自带。
- `Spritesheet/`：角色、物件、道具、UI 图集。

**用什么工具组合成带物理边界的地图**

最推荐这 3 种：

1. **Tiled Map Editor**
   官网：[https://www.mapeditor.org/](https://www.mapeditor.org/)

   适合：用这些 Kenney 素材拼完整 2D 地图。

   它可以：
   - 导入 tilesheet
   - 画地面、道路、建筑、墙
   - 分图层：Ground / Objects / Collision / Events
   - 给 tile 或对象画 collision shape
   - 导出 `.tmx`、`.json`
   - 被 Phaser、Godot、Unity 等读取

   对 Web 游戏最适合：`Tiled + Phaser`。

2. **Godot 4 TileMap / TileSet**
   适合：直接在 Godot 里做可玩地图。

   它可以：
   - 导入 PNG tilesheet
   - 创建 `TileSet`
   - 给墙、水、建筑加碰撞
   - 用 `TileMapLayer` 画地图
   - 直接运行、调试、导出桌面/手机/web

   对你当前 `GameGmeta` 最适合：`Godot 4 + TileMapLayer`。

3. **Tilesetter**
   适合：自动生成地形边缘和 auto-tile。

   它更像 tileset 辅助工具，不是完整游戏关卡编辑器。  
   它可以帮你把草地、水边、道路边缘生成自动拼接规则，但最后还是要进 Tiled / Godot / Phaser 使用。

**我建议的实际路线**

如果目标是 **Web 端游戏**：

```text
Kenney PNG tilesheet
-> Tiled Map Editor 拼完整地图
-> 设置 Collision 图层 / Object Layer
-> 导出 JSON
-> Phaser 读取地图
-> 玩家和 NPC 按 collision 边界移动
```

如果目标是 **Godot + Web/桌面/手机都可导出**：

```text
Kenney PNG tilesheet
-> Godot TileSet
-> TileMapLayer 画地图
-> 在 TileSet 里给墙、水、建筑设置 Physics Layer
-> CharacterBody2D 玩家移动
-> Godot 导出 Web
```

**当前项目里的问题**

现在 Web 版使用的是 `Sample.png` 作为完整地图背景图。  
这适合展示和选择地图，但 **不能自动知道哪里是墙、哪里是水、哪里能走**。

要做成真正可玩的带物理边界地图，需要下一步改成：

- 使用 `tilesheet + tilemap data`
- 或手工建立 `collision layer`
- 或读取 `.tmx/.json` 地图文件

**推荐下一步**

对 `GameGmeta`，我建议优先用：

```text
Tiled Map Editor + Phaser
```

原因：
- Web 游戏最自然。
- Kenney 很多包已经自带 `.tmx` / `.tsx` 示例。
- Tiled 能直接画碰撞层。
- Phaser 原生支持 Tiled JSON tilemap。
- 比手写 canvas 碰撞更稳。

## [USER]
地图还是没有设计感，如何让地图有设计感

## [USER]
地图还是没有设计感，如何让地图有设计感

## [ASSISTANT]
现在的问题不是 Godot 技术链路，而是地图还在“程序摆矩形”。TileSet/TileMapLayer 只是工具，要有设计感需要先做地图构图、区域功能和可走路径，再把 tile 当画笔去铺。

## [ASSISTANT]
现在的问题不是 Godot 技术链路，而是地图还在“程序摆矩形”。TileSet/TileMapLayer 只是工具，要有设计感需要先做地图构图、区域功能和可走路径，再把 tile 当画笔去铺。

## [ASSISTANT]
现在地图“技术对了，但设计不对”。原因是它还是按代码把路、房子、水、树摆成矩形，缺少地图设计里的 **构图、层次、动线、地标、过渡 tile 和细节密度**。

要有设计感，需要这样做：

1. **先做构图，不先铺 tile**
   每张地图先确定：
   - 玩家出生点在哪里
   - 主路怎么引导玩家移动
   - 地标在哪里，比如广场、城堡、主建筑、水渠、入口
   - 哪些地方可走，哪些地方是视觉边界
   - 画面中心看起来是否有主题

2. **参考图只当“构图蓝图”**
   不能把参考图切成 runtime 地图，但可以按参考图复刻结构：
   - 城市：十字路口、街区、建筑群、人行道、公园、停车位
   - 村庄：自然小路、房屋院落、篱笆、树林、城堡入口
   - 世界图：岛屿轮廓、路线节点、水体边界、目标点

3. **不要只用单一中心 tile**
   当前最大问题是大面积重复 tile。应该使用：
   - 道路边缘、道路中心、转角、交叉口
   - 水面中心、水岸边缘、内外角
   - 草地基础、草地杂点、泥地过渡
   - 建筑屋顶、墙体、门窗、阴影、台阶

4. **做“地图组件库”**
   不是每次手写矩形，而是做一组可复用 stamp：
   - `city_crossroad`
   - `city_shop_7x8`
   - `city_park`
   - `village_house_yard`
   - `village_castle_gate`
   - `farm_fence_area`
   - `water_with_shore`
   - `forest_cluster`

5. **物理边界必须跟视觉一致**
   有碰撞的东西必须是玩家一眼看得懂的：
   - 墙、建筑、水、树、篱笆、岩石可以撞
   - 路面、草地、小装饰不要乱挡
   - 不要出现“看起来能走但撞墙”的地方

6. **用 Godot 的正确制作方式**
   最适合的流程是：
   - 在 `TileSet` 里创建 Atlas
   - 给道路/水/草地配置 Terrain Set 或至少准备边缘 tile
   - 在 `TileMapLayer` 里手工或半自动铺地图
   - 给建筑、水、墙、树设置 Physics Layer
   - 代码只负责加载地图、角色、相机、切换卡片

我的建议是下一步不要继续微调现在这套矩形生成器，而是先把 **一张城市地图** 做成“参考级样板”：先按参考图重建街区、路口、建筑群和公园，再把这个方法复制到村庄/世界/塔防地图。这样地图才会从“技术 demo”变成“有美术设计的地图”。

从图片视觉理解上看地图，有哪些地方的元素组合不合理

## [USER]
从图片视觉理解上看地图，有哪些地方的元素组合不合理

## [ASSISTANT]
我先按当前浏览器截图做一次纯视觉审查，不从代码角度解释。重点看“元素之间是否有场景逻辑”：门前是否可达、树是否像自然生长、道路是否服务建筑、水/陆边界是否像地图而不是拼贴。

## [ASSISTANT]
我先按当前浏览器截图做一次纯视觉审查，不从代码角度解释。重点看“元素之间是否有场景逻辑”：门前是否可达、树是否像自然生长、道路是否服务建筑、水/陆边界是否像地图而不是拼贴。

## [ASSISTANT]
从视觉上看，当前地图还有这些不合理点：

**城市图**
- 建筑像“贴在地面上的楼块”，缺少演示图里的完整街区关系：建筑前应有连续人行道、入口广场、路灯/垃圾桶/标牌配套。
- 楼门窗已经有了，但底部门窗太规则，所有建筑像同一个模板重复，缺少不同店铺门面组合。
- 公园和水池离道路关系还弱：水池/草地应该被人行道包围，当前更像嵌在灰地里的绿色矩形。
- 车辆只是在道路上出现，缺少车道、停车位、路口等待区的语义。

**村庄图**
- 房屋+围栏太机械：每个房子都是方形围栏院子，演示图里更多是开放草地、短围栏、门前小路，不是每户完整围住。
- 树林边界还是太规则，像按坐标摆放；演示图里的树是沿地图边缘自然聚集，中间只少量单树。
- 房屋朝向一致、尺寸一致，村庄缺少主次关系：应有主屋、小屋、城堡/井/公告牌形成中心。
- 草地装饰和花散得太均匀，缺少“房前、路旁、林边”这种位置逻辑。

**世界岛屿图**
- 岛岸线还偏几何，边缘像一圈规则贴边块；自然岛屿应有凹凸、沙滩/泥地过渡、港口或登陆点。
- 岛上的房子像孤立贴图，缺少村庄区域、道路入口、码头或资源点支撑。
- 路径和标记点偏抽象，不像真实地形路线，更像 UI 节点图。
- 树木分布没有地貌逻辑，应该集中在林地/山脚/岛边，不应平均分散。

**科幻图**
- 科幻资产和 Tiny Town 泥地/Urban 灰路混合后风格有冲突；应该更多使用 sci-fi pack 自带的地面、道路、基地元素。
- 基地建筑散落，缺少功能分区：主基地、电站、矿区、车库、载具停放区之间没有明确关系。
- 道路太像普通灰色铺装，不像演示图里的科幻荒地弯曲道路。
- 大型建筑和小角色/载具比例还不统一，视觉上有些“图标摆在地图上”的感觉。

下一步最应该改的是：村庄去掉大量完整方形围栏，城市改成真正街区，世界图重做岛岸线和港口，科幻图统一用 sci-fi 包的道路/基地语义。

## [USER]
方案讨论，如何生成图片中相同的地图，可行方法

## [USER]
方案讨论，如何生成图片中相同的地图，可行方法

## [ASSISTANT]
可行，分 3 个等级：

**1. 视觉上完全一样，最快**
直接裁切左侧完整地图作为一张背景图放进 Godot，然后手动画/自动生成碰撞层。

优点：最像原图，最快。  
缺点：不是 TileMapLayer 拼出来的地图，不能单独替换树、房子、水、桥等 tile。

适合：先做可玩的地图验证。

**2. TileMap 方式复刻，推荐**
把右侧素材表裁成独立 tile/stamp，再按左侧地图重新组合。

流程：

1. 裁切左侧完整地图和右侧素材表。
2. 判断缩放比例：这张图大概率是 16px tile 被放大到 32px 显示。
3. 建立素材库：
   - 地面 tile：草地、沙地、花草、悬崖、水边。
   - 路径 tile：泥路直线、转角、交叉。
   - 大对象 stamp：房子、城堡、桥、农场围栏、树丛、山洞。
4. 在 Godot 里分层：
   - `GroundTileMapLayer`：草地、沙地、泥路。
   - `WaterTileMapLayer`：河流、湖泊、海岸。
   - `ObjectTileMapLayer`：房屋、城堡、桥、树、山石。
   - `DecorTileMapLayer`：花、草、牌子、NPC、箱子。
   - `Physics Layer`：水、树、房屋、城堡、山、围栏碰撞。
5. 按左侧地图画出语义结构：
   - 左上森林 + 城堡。
   - 中间主路 S 形穿过地图。
   - 左中房子与河流。
   - 右上农场围栏。
   - 中下桥跨河。
   - 右下山洞/岩石区。
   - 底部沙滩和海水。

优点：是真正的 TileMap，可编辑、可碰撞、可扩展。  
缺点：要手工/半自动复刻，不能一键完全一样。

**3. 自动识别生成，最复杂**
用脚本把左侧地图按网格切成 tile，再和右侧素材表做像素匹配，自动生成 TileMap 数据。

可行但有难点：

- 房子、城堡、树丛是多 tile 对象，不能只按单格匹配。
- 有些对象有透明边缘、阴影、叠层。
- 地图里存在覆盖关系：草地上有花，路上有人，水边有悬崖。
- 需要先分层识别：地面、水、建筑、装饰、角色。

推荐做法是半自动：脚本先裁切和生成候选 tile，然后人工确认关键 stamp。

我建议采用 **方案 2**：用右侧素材裁成 stamp，在 Godot 里用 TileMapLayer + 多层对象组合，复刻左侧地图结构。这样既接近原图，又保留真实地图碰撞和后续编辑能力。

## [ASSISTANT]
这个 JSON 使用的是独立 PNG 资产 ID，不是单张 atlas 坐标；所以最稳的 Godot 生成方式是：把 JSON 作为地图数据文件保存，Godot 运行时按 manifest 找到每个 PNG，生成 Sprite2D 网格层，同时把非地形大物件生成 StaticBody2D 碰撞。这样能直接使用你在网页编辑器导出的 JSON。

## [USER]
可行讨论，目前最先进的游戏像素地图Ai制作方法是什么

## [USER]
可行讨论，目前最先进的游戏像素地图Ai制作方法是什么

## [ASSISTANT]
目前最可行、最先进的像素地图 AI 制作方法不是“让 AI 直接画一张地图图”，而是 **AI 生成结构化地图数据 + 程序约束验证 + 引擎内 TileMap 重建**。

**推荐路线**
1. **素材语义化**
   把 Kenney 素材库变成可检索资产表：
   `tile id / 类型 / 可连接方向 / 是否可走 / 是否碰撞 / 所属包 / 适用场景`
   例如草地、水岸、道路、墙、房屋、树、桥、装饰物分别有语义标签。

2. **AI 先生成地图意图，不直接摆图**
   用 LLM 生成类似：
   ```json
   {
     "biomes": ["village", "forest", "pond"],
     "paths": [{"from":"gate","to":"house"}],
     "zones": [{"type":"farm","rect":[20,8,8,6]}],
     "rules": ["house must touch path", "water needs edge tiles"]
   }
   ```

3. **约束求解生成 tilemap**
   用 WFC、规则系统、自动边缘匹配生成真实 tile 网格。WFC 适合“参考图学习相邻关系”，Godot 也有 WFC 插件可用。它比纯随机更适合保持素材包原本的组合语义。

4. **AI 只做高层规划，程序负责正确性**
   AI 决定“村庄、农田、水池、森林在哪里”；程序负责：
   - 水边缘必须闭合
   - 道路必须连通
   - 房屋门口必须接路
   - 墙、水、建筑设置碰撞
   - 玩家出生点必须可达

5. **导出到 Godot**
   最后生成：
   - `TileSet`
   - `TileMapLayer`
   - collision layer
   - navigation layer
   - object/stamp layer
   - spawn points

**为什么不是直接用图像 AI**
图像 AI 可以生成漂亮参考图，但问题是：没有 tile ID、没有碰撞、没有可编辑层、边缘不一定可拼接。它适合做“参考图/美术方向”，不适合直接作为游戏地图数据。

**当前最适合我们项目的方案**
基于现有编辑器继续升级：

- Web 端：Kenney 素材库 + 拖拽编辑 + JSON 导出
- AI 端：输入“生成一个村庄地图”，输出结构化 JSON
- 规则端：自动修正水岸、道路、围栏、建筑门口
- Godot 端：读取 JSON，生成 `TileMapLayer + Physics`

这比现在手动拖拽更进一步，也比单纯 AI 画图更可落地。  
LVLFORGE 这类工具也在走“文本 + tileset -> Godot tilemap”的方向；Godot 官方 TileMap 体系本身支持 TileSet、碰撞、导航等层级，这是最终应该落地的格式。

Sources: [Godot TileMaps](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/4.0/tutorials/2d/using_tilemaps.html), [LVLFORGE](https://www.lvlforge.com/), [Godot WFC plugin](https://godotengine.org/asset-library/asset/2473), [Scenario Retro Diffusion Tile](https://www.scenario.com/models/retro-diffusion-tile), [GameTileNet](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02941)

## [DEVELOPER]
<permissions instructions>
Filesystem sandboxing defines which files can be read or written. `sandbox_mode` is `workspace-write`: The sandbox permits reading files, and editing files in `cwd` and `writable_roots`. Editing files in other directories requires approval. Network access is restricted.
# Escalation Requests

Commands are run outside the sandbox if they are approved by the user, or match an existing rule that allows it to run unrestricted. The command string is split into independent command segments at shell control operators, including but not limited to:

- Pipes: |
- Logical operators: &&, ||
- Command separators: ;
- Subshell boundaries: (...), $(...)

Each resulting segment is evaluated independently for sandbox restrictions and approval requirements.

Example:

git pull | tee output.txt

This is treated as two command segments:

["git", "pull"]

["tee", "output.txt"]

Commands that use more advanced shell features like redirection (>, >>, <), substitutions ($(...), ...), environment variables (FOO=bar), or wildcard patterns (*, ?) will not be evaluated against rules, to limit the scope of what an approved rule allows.

## How to request escalation

IMPORTANT: To request approval to execute a command that will require escalated privileges:

- Provide the `sandbox_permissions` parameter with the value `"require_escalated"`
- Include a short question asking the user if they want to allow the action in `justification` parameter. e.g. "Do you want to download and install dependencies for this project?"
- Optionally suggest a `prefix_rule` - this will be shown to the user with an option to persist the rule approval for future sessions.

If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with "require_escalated". ALWAYS proceed to use the `justification` parameter - do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.

## When to request escalation

While commands are running inside the sandbox, here are some scenarios that will require escalation outside the sandbox:

- You need to run a command that writes to a directory that requires it (e.g. running tests that write to /var)
- You need to run a GUI app (e.g., open/xdg-open/osascript) to open browsers or files.
- If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with `require_escalated`. ALWAYS proceed to use the `sandbox_permissions` and `justification` parameters. do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.
- You are about to take a potentially destructive action such as an `rm` or `git reset` that the user did not explicitly ask for.
- Be judicious with escalating, but if completing the user's request requires it, you should do so - don't try and circumvent approvals by using other tools.

## prefix_rule guidance

When choosing a `prefix_rule`, request one that will allow you to fulfill similar requests from the user in the future without re-requesting escalation. It should be categorical and reasonably scoped to similar capabilities. You should rarely pass the entire command into `prefix_rule`.

### Banned prefix_rules 
Avoid requesting overly broad prefixes that the user would be ill-advised to approve. For example, do not request ["python3"], ["python", "-"], or other similar prefixes that would allow arbitrary scripting.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule argument for destructive commands like rm.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule if your command uses a heredoc or herestring. 

### Examples
Good examples of prefixes:
- ["npm", "run", "dev"]
- ["gh", "pr", "check"]
- ["cargo", "test"]

# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images and videos using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When sending or referencing a local image or video, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Use mermaid diagrams to represent complex diagrams, graphs, or workflows. Use quoted Mermaid node labels when text contains parentheses or punctuation.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, call `load_workspace_dependencies` to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Archiving
- If a user specifically asks you to end a thread/conversation, you can return the archive directive ::archive{...} to archive the thread/conversation.
- Example: ::archive{reason="User requested to end conversation"}

During execution: if you hit repeated errors, confusing behavior, or suspect

How to decide whether to verify memory:

- Consider both risk of drift and verification effort.
- If a fact is likely to drift and is cheap to verify, verify it before
  answering.
- If a fact is likely to drift but verification is expensive, slow, or
  disruptive, it is acceptable to answer from memory in an interactive turn,
  but you should say that it is memory-derived, note that it may be stale, and
  consider offering to refresh it live.
- If a fact is lower-drift and cheap to verify, use judgment: verification is
  more important when the fact is central to the answer or especially easy to
  confirm.
- If a fact is lower-drift and expensive to verify, it is usually fine to
  answer from memory directly.

When answering from memory without current verification:

- If you rely on memory for a fact that you did not verify in the current turn,
  say so briefly in the final answer.
- If that fact is plausibly drift-prone or comes from an older note, older
  snapshot, or prior run summary, say that it may be stale or outdated.
- If live verification was skipped and a refresh would be useful in the
  interactive context, consider offering to verify or refresh it live.
- Do not present unverified memory-derived facts as confirmed-current.
- For interactive requests, prefer a short refresh offer over silently doing
  expensive verification that the user did not ask for.
- When the unverified fact is about prior results, commands, timing, or an
  older snapshot, a concrete refresh offer can be especially helpful.

`<oai-mem-citation>` block as the VERY LAST content of the final reply.

`<oai-mem-citation>` block at the end.

</rollout_ids>

```
- `citation_entries` is for rendering:
  - one citation entry per line
  - format: `<file>:<line_start>-<line_end>|note=[<how memory was used>]`
  - use file paths relative to the memory base path (for example, `MEMORY.md`,
    `rollout_summaries/...`, `skills/...`)
  - only cite files actually used under the memory base path (do not cite
    workspace files as memory citations)
  - if you used `MEMORY.md` and then a rollout summary/skill file, cite both
  - list entries in order of importance (most important first)
  - `note` should be short, single-line, and use simple characters only (avoid
    unusual symbols, no newlines)
- `rollout_ids` is for us to track what previous rollouts you find useful:
  - include one rollout id per line
  - rollout ids should look like UUIDs (for example,
    `019c6e27-e55b-73d1-87d8-4e01f1f75043`)
  - include unique ids only; do not repeat ids
  - an empty `<rollout_ids>` section is allowed if no rollout ids are available
  - you can find rollout ids in rollout summary files and MEMORY.md
  - do not include file paths or notes in this section
  - For every `citation_entries`, try to find and cite the corresponding rollout id if possible
- Never include memory citations inside pull-request messages.
- Never cite blank lines; double-check ranges.

## Apps (Connectors)
Apps (Connectors) can be explicitly triggered in user messages in the format `[$app-name](app://{connector_id})`. Apps can also be implicitly triggered as long as the context suggests usage of available apps.
An app is equivalent to a set of MCP tools within the `codex_apps` MCP.
An installed app's MCP tools are either provided to you already, or can be lazy-loaded through the `tool_search` tool. If `tool_search` is available, the apps that are searchable by `tools_search` will be listed by it.
Do not additionally call list_mcp_resources or list_mcp_resource_templates for apps.
</apps_instructions><skills_instructions>

### How to use skills
- Discovery: The list above is the skills available in this session (name + description + short path). Skill bodies live on disk at the listed paths after expanding the matching alias from `### Skill roots`.
- Trigger rules: If the user names a skill (with `$SkillName` or plain text) OR the task clearly matches a skill's description shown above, you must use that skill for that turn. Multiple mentions mean use them all. Do not carry skills across turns unless re-mentioned.
- Missing/blocked: If a named skill isn't in the list or the path can't be read, say so briefly and continue with the best fallback.
- How to use a skill (progressive disclosure):
  1) After deciding to use a skill, expand the listed short `path` with the matching alias from `### Skill roots`, then open its `SKILL.md`. Read only enough to follow the workflow.
  2) When `SKILL.md` references relative paths (e.g., `scripts/foo.py`), resolve them relative to the directory containing that expanded `SKILL.md` first, and only consider other paths if needed.
  3) If `SKILL.md` points to extra folders such as `references/`, load only the specific files needed for the request; don't bulk-load everything.
  4) If `scripts/` exist, prefer running or patching them instead of retyping large code blocks.
  5) If `assets/` or templates exist, reuse them instead of recreating from scratch.
- Coordination and sequencing:
  - If multiple skills apply, choose the minimal set that covers the request and state the order you'll use them.
  - Announce which skill(s) you're using and why (one short line). If you skip an obvious skill, say why.
- Context hygiene:
  - Keep context small: summarize long sections instead of pasting them; only load extra files when needed.
  - Avoid deep reference-chasing: prefer opening only files directly linked from `SKILL.md` unless you're blocked.
  - When variants exist (frameworks, providers, domains), pick only the relevant reference file(s) and note that choice.
- Safety and fallback: If a skill can't be applied cleanly (missing files, unclear instructions), state the issue, pick the next-best approach, and continue.

## Plugins
A plugin is a local bundle of skills, MCP servers, and apps. Below is the list of plugins that are enabled and available in this session.
### Available plugins
- `Browser`: Browser / browser-use plugin Aliases: @browser, @browser-use, browser-use, Browser, in-app browser. Use Browser, the Codex in-app browser, when the user asks to open, inspect, navigate, test, click, type, or screenshot local web targets such as localhost, 127.0.0.1, ::1, file:// URLs, or the current in-app browser tab. After significant frontend changes to a local app, use Browser to open the relevant local target when it is known or obvious, unless the user asks for another browser tool. For requests like "open localhost:3000" or "open to localhost:4000", navigate the in-app browser to http://localhost:3000 or http://localhost:4000. Do not satisfy explicit @browser or @browser-use requests with macOS `open`, shell commands, or generic web browsing unless the user asks for another browser tool or approves a fallback.
- `Build iOS Apps`: Build iOS apps with workflows for App Intents, SwiftUI UI work, refactors, performance profiling, leak investigation, and simulator debugging.
- `Build macOS Apps`: Build, run, test, debug, instrument, and implement local macOS apps using Xcode, SwiftUI, AppKit interop, unified logging, and shell-first desktop workflows.
- `Build Web Apps`: Build web apps with frontend asset design, browser testing, UI components, payments, and database guidance.
- `Chrome`: Chrome automation for remote URLs, authenticated/profile-dependent pages, existing Chrome tabs, cookies, extensions, and Codex Chrome Extension setup.
- `CircleCI`: Build, test, and deploy any application
- `Cloudflare`: Cloudflare platform plugin with curated skills for Workers, Wrangler, and Agents SDK plus the official Cloudflare API MCP server.
- `CodeRabbit`: AI-powered code review in Codex, powered by CodeRabbit.
- `Computer Use`: Control desktop apps on macOS from Codex through Computer Use.
- `Documents`: Create and edit document artifacts in Codex, including Word files and Google Docs.
- `Game Studio`: Design, prototype, and ship browser games with guided 2D and 3D workflows, asset pipelines, and playtesting support.
- `GitHub`: Inspect repositories, triage pull requests and issues, debug CI, and publish changes through a hybrid GitHub connector and CLI workflow.
- `Gmail`: Work with Gmail using the configured Gmail app connector.
- `Presentations`: Create, edit, render, verify, and export presentation slide decks. Use when Codex needs to build or modify a deck, slidedeck, presentation deck, slide deck, slides, PowerPoint, Google Slides, PPT, PPTX, .ppt, or .pptx file.
- `Spreadsheets`: Create, edit, analyze, visualize, render, and export spreadsheets or Google Sheets-ready workbooks in Codex.
- `Stripe`: Payments and business tools
- `Test Android Apps`: Test Android apps with emulator workflows for reproduction, screenshots, UI inspection, log capture, and performance profiling.
### How to use plugins
- Discovery: The list above is the plugins available in this session.
- Skill naming: If a plugin contributes skills, those skill entries are prefixed with `plugin_name:` in the Skills list.
- Trigger rules: If the user explicitly names a plugin, prefer capabilities associated with that plugin for that turn.
- Relationship to capabilities: Plugins are not invoked directly. Use their underlying skills, MCP tools, and app tools to help solve the task.
- Preference: When a relevant plugin is available, prefer using capabilities associated with that plugin over standalone capabilities that provide similar functionality.
- Missing/blocked: If the user requests a plugin that is not listed above, or the plugin does not have relevant callable capabilities for the task, say so briefly and continue with the best fallback.

Filesystem sandboxing defines which files can be read or written. `sandbox_mode` is `workspace-write`: The sandbox permits reading files, and editing files in `cwd` and `writable_roots`. Editing files in other directories requires approval. Network access is restricted.
# Escalation Requests

Commands are run outside the sandbox if they are approved by the user, or match an existing rule that allows it to run unrestricted. The command string is split into independent command segments at shell control operators, including but not limited to:

- Pipes: |
- Logical operators: &&, ||
- Command separators: ;
- Subshell boundaries: (...), $(...)

Each resulting segment is evaluated independently for sandbox restrictions and approval requirements.

Example:

git pull | tee output.txt

This is treated as two command segments:

["git", "pull"]

["tee", "output.txt"]

Commands that use more advanced shell features like redirection (>, >>, <), substitutions ($(...), ...), environment variables (FOO=bar), or wildcard patterns (*, ?) will not be evaluated against rules, to limit the scope of what an approved rule allows.

## How to request escalation

IMPORTANT: To request approval to execute a command that will require escalated privileges:

- Provide the `sandbox_permissions` parameter with the value `"require_escalated"`
- Include a short question asking the user if they want to allow the action in `justification` parameter. e.g. "Do you want to download and install dependencies for this project?"
- Optionally suggest a `prefix_rule` - this will be shown to the user with an option to persist the rule approval for future sessions.

If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with "require_escalated". ALWAYS proceed to use the `justification` parameter - do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.

## When to request escalation

While commands are running inside the sandbox, here are some scenarios that will require escalation outside the sandbox:

- You need to run a command that writes to a directory that requires it (e.g. running tests that write to /var)
- You need to run a GUI app (e.g., open/xdg-open/osascript) to open browsers or files.
- If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with `require_escalated`. ALWAYS proceed to use the `sandbox_permissions` and `justification` parameters. do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.
- You are about to take a potentially destructive action such as an `rm` or `git reset` that the user did not explicitly ask for.
- Be judicious with escalating, but if completing the user's request requires it, you should do so - don't try and circumvent approvals by using other tools.

## prefix_rule guidance

When choosing a `prefix_rule`, request one that will allow you to fulfill similar requests from the user in the future without re-requesting escalation. It should be categorical and reasonably scoped to similar capabilities. You should rarely pass the entire command into `prefix_rule`.

### Banned prefix_rules 
Avoid requesting overly broad prefixes that the user would be ill-advised to approve. For example, do not request ["python3"], ["python", "-"], or other similar prefixes that would allow arbitrary scripting.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule argument for destructive commands like rm.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule if your command uses a heredoc or herestring. 

### Examples
Good examples of prefixes:
- ["npm", "run", "dev"]
- ["gh", "pr", "check"]
- ["cargo", "test"]
